Appendix A
Technical Lemmas

A.1
Technical Lemmas for Section
3.2

Lemma A.1
.

Let
L\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfy Assumption
W2
.
Moreover, let
\varrho,\widetilde{\varrho}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Then for any
\beta\in(0,1]
it holds

\left|\int_{\beta/2}^{\beta}q^{L}_{a}[\varrho]da-\int_{\beta/2}^{\beta}q^{L}_{a}[\widetilde{\varrho}]da\right|\leq C_{\beta,L}W_{2}\left(\varrho,\widetilde{\varrho}\right),

(A.1)

where
q^{L}_{a}[\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet}]
is defined in (
2.4
) and
C_{\beta,L}
is a constant depending on
\beta
and Lipschitz coefficient
C_{2}
defined in Assumption
W2
.

Proof.

One can compute that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\beta/2}^{\beta}q^{L}_{a}[\varrho]da-\int_{\beta/2}^{\beta}q^{L}_{a}[\widetilde{\varrho}]da\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left|q_{a}^{L}[\varrho]-q_{a}^{L}[\widetilde{\varrho}]\right|^{2}da\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(A.2)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{2}}W_{2}\left(L_{\sharp}\varrho,L_{\sharp}\widetilde{\varrho}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{2}}W_{2}\left(\varrho,\widetilde{\varrho}\right),

where in the equality we have used the fact that in one dimension the Wasserstein distance between two measures can be computed in terms of the
L^{2}
-distance between their quantile functions, and in the last line we have used the fact that
L
is Lipschitz.
∎

A.2
Technical Lemmas for Section
3.3

Lemma A.2
.

Let
L\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
G\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfy
W4
and
W6
.
Moreover, let
\varrho\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfy
\int\left\|{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}d\varrho\leq K
and
\varrho(Q^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho])\geq c
with
K<\infty
and
c>0
.
Then

\frac{\exp(-\alpha\makebox[0.0pt]{$\phantom{G}\mkern 1.5mu\underline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{G}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$}G)}{\left\|{\omega_{\alpha}^{G}}\right\|_{L^{1}(I^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho])}}\leq\frac{1}{c}\exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{c}C_{1}(1+K)\right)=:c_{K}.

(A.3)

Proof.

By the definitions of
I^{L}_{\beta}[\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet}]
and
Q^{L}_{\beta}[\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet}]
in (
2.2
) and (
2.3
), one can compute that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-\alpha\left(G(\theta)-\makebox[0.0pt]{$\phantom{G}\mkern 1.5mu\underline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{G}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$}G\right)\right)dI^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho](\theta)

\displaystyle=\varrho\left(Q^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho]\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\varrho\left(Q^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho]\right)}\exp\left(-\alpha\left(G(\theta)-\makebox[0.0pt]{$\phantom{G}\mkern 1.5mu\underline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{G}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$}G\right)\right)dI^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho](\theta)

(A.4)

\displaystyle\geq\varrho\left(Q^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho]\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{\varrho\left(Q^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho]\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(G(\theta)-\makebox[0.0pt]{$\phantom{G}\mkern 1.5mu\underline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{G}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$}G\right)dI^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho](\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\geq c\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(G(\theta)-\makebox[0.0pt]{$\phantom{G}\mkern 1.5mu\underline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{G}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$}G)dI^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho](\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\geq c\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}C_{1}\left(1+\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\right)dI^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho](\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\geq c\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}C_{1}\left(1+\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\right)d\varrho(\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\geq c\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{c}C_{1}(1+K)\right).

Here the first inequality uses Jensen’s inequality, and for the second and third inequalities we use assumption
\varrho(Q^{L}_{\beta}[\varrho])\geq c
and the growth condition
W6
for
G
, respectively.
We conclude the proof by reordering the above computations.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Let the dimension
d\geq 3
and let
\widetilde{\varrho}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Then, for any
r>0
and any
\varrho\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
it holds

\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}d\theta\leq C\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}^{2}\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(A.5)

for a constant
C=C(d,r)
.

Proof.

First case:
\|m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}\leq r+1
.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}\frac{\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}}{\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}}d\theta

(A.6)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\frac{1}{\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}^{2}\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{B_{2r+1}(0)}\frac{1}{\left\|\theta\right\|_{2}^{2}}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}^{2}\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{2r+1}\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\frac{1}{r^{2}}dS\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}^{2}\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\text{Vol}\left(S^{d-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{(2r+1)^{d-2}}{d-2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}^{2}\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\text{Vol}(S^{d-1})
denotes the volume of unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Second case:
\|m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}>r+1
.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}d\theta

\displaystyle\leq\text{Vol}(B_{r}(0))^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(A.7)

\displaystyle\leq\text{Vol}(B_{r}(0))^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B_{r}(0)}\|\nabla\varrho\|_{2}\|\theta-m(\widetilde{\varrho})\|_{2}^{2}d\theta\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

Lemma A.4
.

Given fixed
T>0
,
\lambda,\sigma>0
, and
u\in\mathcal{C}\left([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, let
\rho
be the solution to the following linear Fokker-Planck equation

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}=\lambda\nabla\cdot\left(\left(\theta-u_{t}\right)\rho_{t}\right)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\Delta\left(\left\|\theta-u_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho_{t}\right)

(A.8)

in the weak sense with
\rho_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Then there exists a constant
C=C(T,d,\lambda,\sigma,\lVert u\rVert_{C([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})})>0
such that
\|\rho_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

For any
2\leq p<\infty
, we can compute that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{d}{dt}\|\rho\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial_{t}\rho_{t},\rho_{t}^{p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(A.9)

\displaystyle=\lambda\int\nabla\cdot\left(\left(\theta-u_{t}\right)\rho_{t}\right)\rho_{t}^{p-1}d\theta+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\int\Delta\left(\left\|\theta-u_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho_{t}\right)\rho_{t}^{p-1}d\theta

\displaystyle=-\lambda(p-1)\int\left(\theta-u_{t}\right)^{T}\nabla\rho_{t}\rho_{t}^{p-1}d\theta-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}(p-1)\int\nabla\left(\left\|\theta-u_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho_{t}\right)^{T}\nabla\rho_{t}\rho_{t}^{p-2}d\theta

\displaystyle=-(p-1)\left(\lambda+\sigma^{2}\right)\int\left(\theta-u_{t}\right)^{T}\nabla\rho_{t}\rho_{t}^{p-1}d\theta-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}(p-1)\int\left\|\theta-u_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho_{t}^{p-2}d\theta

\displaystyle=-(p-1)\left(\lambda+\sigma^{2}\right)\int\left(\theta-u_{t}\right)^{T}\nabla\left(\frac{1}{p}\rho_{t}^{p}\right)d\theta-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}(p-1)\int\left\|\theta-u_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho_{t}^{p-2}d\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{p-1}{p}d\left(\lambda+\sigma^{2}\right)\int|\rho_{t}|^{p}d\theta-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}(p-1)\int\left\|\theta-u_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho_{t}^{p-2}d\theta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p-1}{p}d\left(\lambda+\sigma^{2}\right)\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

By Grönwall’s inequality, we know that

\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\exp\left(\frac{p-1}{p}d(\lambda+\sigma^{2})t\right)\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(A.10)

Therefore, for any
t\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\exp\left(\frac{p-1}{p}d(\lambda+\sigma^{2})t\right)\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(A.11)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(d(\lambda+\sigma^{2})t\right)\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(d(\lambda+\sigma^{2})T\right)\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\{\rho_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in[0,T]}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\|\rho_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C
with a uniform constant
C>0
for any
\tau\in[0,T]
. Suppose that for every
s
and every
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we have

\lim_{t\rightarrow s}\int\phi(\theta)\rho_{t}(\theta)d\theta=\int\phi(\theta)\rho_{s}(\theta)d\theta.

Then, for every
s\in[0,T]
we have that
\rho_{t}
converges weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
towards
\rho_{s}
as
t\rightarrow s
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for any
\phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a function
\psi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\|\phi-\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\varepsilon/2
.
Applying the triangle inequality, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the fact that
\|\rho_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C
uniformly for all
\tau\in[0,T]
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\int\phi(\theta)\rho_{t}(\theta)d\theta-\int\phi(\theta)\rho_{s}(\theta)d\theta\right|

(A.12)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\left(\phi(\theta)-\psi(\theta)\right)\rho_{t}(\theta)d\theta\right|+\left|\int\psi(\theta)\left(\rho_{t}(\theta)-\rho_{s}(\theta)\right)d\theta\right|+\left|\int\left(\phi(\theta)-\psi(\theta)\right)\rho_{s}(\theta)d\theta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\phi-\psi\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left(\|\rho_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\rho_{s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)+\left|\int\psi(\theta)\left(\rho_{t}(\theta)-\rho_{s}(\theta)\right)d\theta\right|

\displaystyle<C\varepsilon+\left|\int\psi(\theta)\left(\rho_{t}(\theta)-\rho_{s}(\theta)\right)d\theta\right|.

The latter term in the last line above converges to
0
as
t\rightarrow s
by the assumption and the fact
\psi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, implying that

\limsup_{t\rightarrow s}\left|\int\phi(\theta)\rho_{t}(\theta)d\theta-\int\phi(\theta)\rho_{s}(\theta)d\theta\right|\leq C\varepsilon,

(A.13)

which implies the desired result since
\varepsilon
was arbitrary.
∎

The following is a standard fact from functional analysis.

Lemma A.6
.

For
l\geq 1
, let
\{u^{n}\}_{n}\subset W^{1,\infty}([0,T],H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
be a sequence of functions with a uniformly bounded norm in this space.
Suppose
u
is such that
u^{n}\rightarrow u
strongly in
\mathcal{C}([0,T],L_{\text{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
Then
u
has the regularity
u\in W^{1,\infty}([0,T],H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

Proof.

By the Banach-Alaoglu theorem
[
87
]
(applied in the Banach space
W^{1,\infty}([0,T],H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
), there exists a subsequence of
u^{n}
(not relabeled) and a function
\tilde{u}
such that

u^{n}\rightharpoonup^{*}\tilde{u}\qquad\text{in}\;\;W^{1,\infty}([0,T],H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\,.

That is, for any test function
\phi\in L^{1}([0,T],H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, we have

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\langle u^{n},\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\tilde{u},\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt\,,

(A.14a)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}u^{n},\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{u},\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt\,.

(A.14b)

Our goal now is to show that
\tilde{u}=u
. In turn, it will suffice to show that
\tilde{u}\xi=u\xi
for any arbitrary smooth cutoff function
\chi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Let
v^{n}(t,\theta):=\chi(\theta)u^{n}(t,\theta)
and
v(t,\theta):=\chi(\theta)\tilde{u}(t,\theta)
. From the strong convergence of
u^{n}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{C}([0,T],L_{\text{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, we know that
v^{n}=\chi u^{n}\rightarrow\chi u
strongly in
\mathcal{C}([0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
Thus, together with (
A.14a
), we obtain, for any
\phi\in L^{1}([0,T],H^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,

\int_{0}^{T}\langle v,\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\langle v^{n},\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\chi u,\phi(t,\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}dt.

Using a density argument, the above implies that
v=\chi u
, finishing with this the proof.

∎

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\{u^{n}\}_{n}\subset L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a sequence of functions with a uniformly bounded norm in this space.
Suppose
u
is such that
u^{n}\rightarrow u
strongly in
\mathcal{C}([0,T],L_{\text{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
Then
u
has the regularity
u\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

Proof.

Since
u^{n}
strongly converges to
u
in
\mathcal{C}([0,T],L_{\text{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, it follows that for any fixed compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, up to subsequence (not relabeled), we have

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}u^{n}(t,x)=u(t,x)\qquad\text{for a.e. }(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K\,.

Thus, we conclude that

|u(t,x)|=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}|u^{n}(t,x)|\leq\sup_{n}\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<+\infty

for a.e.
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
.
By the arbitrariness of
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we conclude that
u\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
∎

Lemma A.8
.

Let
\rho\in L^{2}\left([0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\right)
satisfy

1.

\lim_{t\rightarrow s}\|\rho_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|\rho_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
for any
s\in[0,T]
.

2.

For any
s\in[0,T]
,
\rho_{t}

weakly converges to
\rho_{s}
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
t\rightarrow s
, i.e.

\lim_{t\rightarrow s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(\theta)\rho_{t}(\theta)d\theta=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(\theta)\rho_{s}(\theta)d\theta,

(A.15)

for every
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Then
\rho_{t}
strongly converges to
\rho_{s}
as
t\rightarrow s
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
In other words,
\rho\in\mathcal{C}\left([0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\right)
.

Proof.

For any
s\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow s}\left\|\rho_{t}-\rho_{s}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow s}\|\rho_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-2\lim_{t\rightarrow s}\langle\rho_{t},\rho_{s}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\rho_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(A.16)

\displaystyle=\|\rho_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-2\|\rho_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\rho_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0,

where the second equality uses the fact that
\phi_{s}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\rho_{t}
converges to
\rho_{s}
weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
∎

Lemma A.9
.

Suppose that
\varrho
is a density function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and that
\{\varrho^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{P}_{4}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a sequence of densities satisfying

1.

\varrho^{n}
strongly converges to
\varrho
in
L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

2.

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\int\|\theta\|_{2}^{4}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\leq C
for some constant
C>0
.

Then
\{\varrho^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\varrho
in the Wasserstein-
2
distance, i.e.,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}W_{2}(\varrho^{n},\varrho)=0.

Proof.

Since weak convergence of probability measures and convergence in second moments implies convergence in the Wasserstein-
2
distance (see, e.g.,
[
95
, Chapter 6]
), we will show that
\varrho^{n}
converges to
\varrho
weakly and that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}d\theta\rightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho d\theta
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

For given
r>0
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\leq\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{4}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\leq\frac{C}{r^{2}}.

(A.17)

Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we pick
r_{\varepsilon}=\sqrt{\frac{C}{\varepsilon}}
, which ensures that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\leq\varepsilon
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
From Lemma
A.10
, we know that
\int\|\theta\|_{2}^{4}\varrho d\theta\leq C
.
Then the same computation in (
A.17
) holds for
\varrho
, and we also have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho(\theta)d\theta\leq\varepsilon
.
Then one can compute

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}

(A.18)

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\left(\varrho^{n}(\theta)-\varrho(\theta)\right)d\theta+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\int_{B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\left(\varrho^{n}(\theta)-\varrho(\theta)\right)d\theta\right|^{2}+2\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\int_{B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{4}d\theta\right)\left\|\varrho^{n}-\varrho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{r_{\varepsilon}(0)})}+4\left(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{r_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{r_{\varepsilon}}\left\|\varrho^{n}-\varrho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{r_{\varepsilon}(0)})}+8\varepsilon^{2}.

By the fact that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\varrho^{n}-\varrho\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{r_{\varepsilon}})}=0
, we have

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}\leq 8\varepsilon^{2}.

(A.19)

By the arbitrariness of
\varepsilon
, we know that
\varrho^{n}
converges to
\varrho
in second moment.
Next we show that
\varrho^{n}
weakly converges to
\varrho
with similar idea.
For any given
\varphi\in C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, denote
M:=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\varphi(\theta)|
.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, with the similar computations as in (
A.17
), we could pick
\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}=\sqrt{\frac{M}{\varepsilon}}
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}|\varphi(\theta)|\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\leq\varepsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}|\varphi(\theta)|\varrho(\theta)d\theta\leq\varepsilon.

(A.20)

Then

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(\theta)\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(\theta)\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}

(A.21)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\int_{B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\varphi(\theta)\left(\varrho^{n}(\theta)-\varrho(\theta)\right)d\theta\right|^{2}+2\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\varphi(\theta)\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\varphi(\theta)\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\int_{B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\varphi(\theta)^{2}d\theta\right)\left\|\varrho^{n}-\varrho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0))}+4\left(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\varphi(\theta)\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0)}\varphi(\theta)\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(r_{\varepsilon},M)\left\|\varrho^{n}-\varrho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{\widetilde{r}_{\varepsilon}}(0))}+8\varepsilon^{2},

which implies that

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(\theta)\varrho^{n}(\theta)d\theta-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(\theta)\varrho(\theta)d\theta\right|^{2}\leq 8\varepsilon^{2}.

(A.22)

Again by the arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
and
\varphi\in C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we obtain that
\varrho^{n}
weakly converges to
\varrho
.
∎

Lemma A.10
.

Let
\varrho
and
\{\varrho^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfy the same assumptions as in Lemma
A.9
.
Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{4}\varrho d\theta\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\theta\|_{2}^{4}\varrho^{n}d\theta.

(A.23)

Proof.

For any fixed
r>0
, we choose a
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
bump function
\psi_{r}
that has support in
B_{2r}(0)
with
|\psi_{r}|\leq 1
and is identically
1
on
B_{r}(0)
.
In particular, we can construct

\psi_{r}(\theta):=1-g\left(\frac{\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}-r^{2}}{3r^{2}}\right)

(A.24)

where

g(x):=\frac{f(x)}{f(x)+f(1-x)}\qquad\text{with}\qquad f(x):=e^{-\frac{1}{x}}\mathds{1}_{\{x>0\}}.

(A.25)

Then one can compute

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{4}\varrho d\theta=\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{4}\psi_{r}(\theta)\varrho d\theta=\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{4}\psi_{r}(\theta)\varrho^{n}d\theta\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{4}\varrho^{n}d\theta.

(A.26)

Here, the first equality is due to the monotone convergence theorem (Theorem of Beppo-Levi) making use of the fact that the sequence
\left\|{\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet}}\right\|_{2}^{4}\psi_{r}(\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\varrho
is monotonously non-decreasing since
\psi_{r}(\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\leq\psi_{\tilde{r}}(\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})
if
0<r\leq\tilde{r}
.
The second equality uses that
\varrho^{n}
strongly converges to
\varrho
in
L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
together with
\left\|{\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet}}\right\|_{2}^{4}\psi_{r}(\mathord{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.67,0.67}\bullet})\in L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for fixed
r>0
.
Eventually, the last inequality just uses that
\psi_{r}\leq 1
.
∎
